High house! ==References====External links== October 1999. ==References== January, sanctions have been imposed against Austria by 14 EU countries, and on 4 March. February – so after that! – Under truly dramatic circumstances, this federal government is fishing, which is now gathered on the government bank. When we hide back what was then said, feared, was written, then it is important to remember that and see where we stand today. It was then feared that Austria would become a kind of "Naziland", Austrian politicians were compared to Hitler, in caricatures the establishment of concentration camps was conjured, the persecution of minorities, refugees, migrants and even a blockade of the enlargement process, a blockade of the Union was feared. Ladies and gentlemen! Rate for yourself what has been real of these fears expressed eight months ago and what we have achieved with one, as I believe, smart and prudent policy! I mean, as an Austrian today, we can say proudly that we have responded sensitively to the fears and concerns expressed at the time and have tried to scare people and not to strengthen fear and anxiety. That's important to me. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] It was not just the government that was affected, ladies and gentlemen – that was also said here. Of course, it was also about the role of the government to exclude democratically elected representatives of a country, but it was much deeper: It was about the attitude of our country to its history. It was about the question of how we are seen from the outside, and also about how we see ourselves, what Austrians say about our country abroad, which picture is being spread. Projects that were important and important have been stopped, student exchange programmes have been temporarily questioned, science projects aborted or questioned, artists are invited. Holiday boycott was threatened – later, thanks to God, he was apologised for it – and overall a wave, a rather hysterical wave, was triggered which caused a counter-reaction in Austria. I also say: It is quite normal and an ancient rule that when a country is attacked, one tries to stand together. Unfortunately, this was not always the case at the beginning. I say this very openly: I regret this, we have made a great deal of effort, and I have invested a lot here too. But I am glad that at the end of this consensus that in a difficult situation, Austria, its democrats, its citizens must stand together, be given and given – for that is important for us! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] This has nothing to do with a Hurra patriotism, ladies and gentlemen, with false patriotism. But I'm not going to stop fighting teeth and claws for the fact that "home", "fatherland" and "patriotism" are terms, which one is still in the 21st century. ==References====External links== [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] I cannot say anything about the origin of the sanctions because I have no first-hand information about them. Those who were and are responsible for this will have to deal with their conscience and certainly also before the history of time. In fact, we in the government have always focused our attention on dealing with the present and, above all, on the future questions for our home country. We didn't get any confidence advance from the first day. On the contrary! You gave us a request for distrust before the presentation of the Government Declaration. We can live with it. As head of government, I have spent more time here than any of my predecessors, because it is important to me as a long-standing parliamentarian that the debate takes place here in this House, in front of the elected representatives of the people, and not outside the street in demonstrations or blockades. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] This is where the debate has to take place! Here you have to prove yourself to be an opposition, and here we have to justify ourselves as a government, as a parliamentarian who supports this government, before the Austrian sovereign, the only one we are really responsible for, namely the Austrian voters – and not any other bodies. This is important to me, and this is also a lesson from these Sanctions months, ladies and gentlemen. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] I said – and I mean it honestly – that we took very seriously the concerns expressed at the beginning in editorial articles, in many emails or letters, in personal conversations and therefore also tried to present a rich theme bouquet of important initiatives in the first six or seven months. These include the improvement of the rights of minorities, the ethnic groups in Austria, the reworking of history, the cleaning of forced labour, the integration efforts and the fight against poverty, which are taken seriously in many areas. You discussed some issues in the early morning. The concerns of the enlargement candidates were also taken seriously by us and, as I believe, treated in a very reasonable way. I would also like to thank you for the fact that, despite the very emotional and controversial debates here in the House, many of these important issues have sometimes been decided unanimously. This is Austrian because I believe that only in a few other European countries would such a great consensus be evident in such a heated emotional discussion. I expressly thank you here at the House. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] We have tried – Mrs. Vice-Chancellor, the whole team and I – to lead our country with discretion and firmness in these months. I had to smile a little, when on Sunday, Cardinal Schönborn, in his remarkable sermon in Mariazell, quoted the great Christian democratic founder of the European Union, former French Foreign Minister Schuman, who had three advices to his political colleagues: Firstly, do not dramatize, second: preserve a certain sense of humor in politics, even if it is difficult, and thirdly, what is even more difficult: If you have to put hits on, don't give them one back to one. – We are not perfect, ladies and gentlemen, and on the third point, I think we all have to work on ourselves. But there are wise advice that Cardinal Schönborn of Schuman, the founder of the Union, has given us here. We want to take care of her. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Now we've been 12 years old. September, 7:00 p.m., the sanctions lifted, they're history. Liberation, a feeling of relief, even joy – I say quite consciously – have filled us. We really enjoyed ourselves together, but especially for the country, because we have our hands back to work in Europe, but of course also in Austria. It also urges me to give some thanks at this point. First: Thanks to many, many Austrians, also to some who have been very skeptical at the beginning, as it will actually succeed, who have seen it then, that deserves confidence, what is happening here from the side of the government. These people have given us a lot of strength and support. Thank you to all those who have given us this advance of confidence that the opposition has refused! [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Second, there are thousands – really thousands! – thanks to EU citizens who encouraged us in letters, in e-mails, in personal discussions and said that they did not believe their governments. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Now the hour of insight is asked: What follows from all of this? – It’s already interesting to read what Romano Prodi says – literally quoted in the last “format” – now: "It won't happen again.", that there is a freezing of bilateral contacts as in the case of Austria. The Danish Foreign Minister, for example, says in the same issue: I personally do not think that we see a reaction like those against Austria again. The Luxembourg Prime Minister was also quoted. He said: I doubt whether we have really done everything right on this question. I therefore think that the hour of insight is good for all of us, including in Austria. We also do not need to break into a triumphal memory, because it is neither necessary nor appropriate, in my opinion, to do so. But the relief we feel should be used as a driving force to focus on the essential tasks in Europe now. What could that be? First: Austria must take its place in the heart of Europe and defend it. Ladies and gentlemen! We will not sacrifice Austrian interests, no one will sacrifice, if he believes that we have to call from Berlin, Paris, Brussels or from elsewhere, what has to happen. Austrian interests are more important than many others that have been called to us in recent weeks. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] This applies to the rights of Austrian water, but this also applies to the safety of nuclear power plants – very interesting in this context is a statement by Romano Prodi, namely: If the applicant countries join, European rules will also apply to their nuclear power plants.; a statement to be heard – and this also applies to the Austrian representation in all European institutions. Secondly, Europe needs a new constitution, a clear division of tasks. It's about: What is to be done at European level, what is to be done nationally, what to solve regionally? – This point must be addressed immediately after the Intergovernmental Conference. Thirdly, It is clear that at a crossroads – and before such a one – fear and uncertainty prevail: the fear of the smaller ones from the greater, the fear of the fasts from the brakes and vice versa, the fear of the old, the established old-members, of enlargement, of the new, the fear of the rich from those who could take away anything from them. What I very much argue for is that if someone expresses criticism of developments, it is being handled with care, for that is far from anti-European. This is an important teaching to draw, for which I very much plead. [[New applause with the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] One should also deal with words such as "Kern" or "Avantgarde" very carefully. I am now quoting someone who has held back remarkably during the last weeks and months, including thanks to the French Foreign Minister Hubert Védrine, who literally explains in an article of the Financial Times: Be careful with concepts such as "hardcore" – that is, core – or "avantgarde", which actually share more than unite. We must put everything aside, which could create new, arbitrary distinctions between Member States. - Quote. Chris Patten, the very considerable Commissioner for Foreign Affairs of the Union – a British – warns against any attempt to lead the Union through a directorate of the large states at the expense of the small or medium-sized ones. This could easily lead to a Europe of the favoured and the disadvantaged. And the candidates should not be allowed to understand that they must eat in the kitchen while the rulers feed on the first floor. - That's an assignment for us. Direct democracy can also have its place in such a Europe. We have meant this seriously with the attempt of a People's Survey, and we are not making this attempt bad – and he has done so too. Not in any case he will be correct and necessary, but he must not be excluded. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and the Freeman.]] Last point: Enlargement is a historic opportunity for us. We therefore want to make the most of active neighbourhood policy in the heart of Europe: through political contacts, through education – there are some Austrian schools in Prague, Budapest, 75 lektorates, Austrian libraries and so on. This active neighbourhood policy can be a asset in Europe in the future. – Thank you for your attention.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-09-20-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00036_d7e6611	Mr President. High house! Ladies and gentlemen! In recent weeks and months, there has been a great deal of talk about European values. A precise and clear definition of these values has never been made, the definition has been nebulos, and above all an important principle has been disregarded, namely that values have to be applied to all, that the scale by which Austria is measured has of course also to be applied to all the others in Europe, and that morality can not only be expected from the others, but also that one has to pay attention to it. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] A morally raised pointing finger against others does not make one or more moral bodies – neither in Europe nor in Austria. And above all, no one can presume to rise by appealing to alleged moral principles to an over-instance over the highest sovereign in Austria, namely the citizens of this country. [[New congratulations to the liberals and the ÖVP.]] It was sobering for many people – not only in Austria, but throughout Europe – to see how easily one has resorted to undemocratic means without reading the spring, supposedly for the protection of democracy. We in Austria – I hope, also in Europe – agree that the top principle of our Constitution is democracy, which itself is the basic prerequisite for becoming a member of the European Union. In Austria, as in any other country, democracy means that the people have the right to elect their own parliament in free and democratic elections and thus also to determine their government. There is no instance – either at national or international level – that can overcome this decision. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] The European Community is an association of 15 equal rights states, sovereign states with equal rights and obligations, whether large or small. Charles de Gaulle, one of the founding fathers of the European Union, declared in 1963: Our approach and then our Union have led us to act together. – Common action and solidarity are the pillars of the European Union that are underpinning and enshrined in the EU Treaty and the political action ceilings of the European integration process. To isolate a government resulting from democratic elections and to give a whole country to the Pranger is a process which is in stark contradiction with these basic principles, namely commonness and solidarity. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] Europe – and I say this in all clarity – does not have the right to impose on Austria or any other member country what government it has to form. [[New congratulations to the liberals and the ÖVP.]] The right to freedom of expression has always been at the heart of discussions in recent days. I would like to stress this right 100% – all of us, the whole federal government, – but I thought it very appropriate that all those who claim the right to express their opinion, of course, should also take the same vehemence for the right to free democratic expression in the form of elections, even if the outcome of the election is not the same as it was desired. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Scatter]] [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] Ladies and gentlemen of the opposition! Respect for the voter's decision is also a European value to be defended. [[New congratulations to the liberals and the ÖVP.]] I say this above all to the address of those who believed that they were unable to prevent or reverse government by pressure from abroad, but also to the address of Heads of Government who believed that they should not respect free elections. Although, ultimately, reason has prevailed and, in accordance with the recommendations of the three wise men, the abolition of EU sanctions against Austria has taken place, developments in recent months require an in-depth analysis and conclusions to be drawn from it. "Austria, the "pattern student" of the European Union," writes Karl-Peter Schwarz in the "Presse", "has experienced painstakingly in recent months, that the EU is not the paradise on earth, that even a society of states, which sees itself as democratic and liberal, can take risks against which to protect itself." - Quote. One of the dangers to be protected against in Europe is the desire to create a Europe of two classes: an avant-garde of a few less large states, a board of directors of a nuclear Europe that wants to determine direction and speed. To spray alertness to the EU's centralist tendencies as backwardness or to defame as chauvinism is gross negligence, which has made clear the events of recent months. Austria – like other small countries in the European Union – will have to play a much more active role in the design of the EU in the future if we do not want to run the risk that our country's existential interests will come under the wheels. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] One of these points is maintaining unanimity on key issues for Austria, such as water resources, spatial planning, land use or the choice of energy sources. [[Inter-calls at the SPÖ.]] In addition, we will have to learn to open discussions on the opportunities and risks of integration and enlargement, Mr. Schieder, without using the "killer argument" of European hostility. [[Abg. Schieder:... shrouds our forest itself!]] [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] Above all, however, we must be aware that there is a deep crisis of trust between the Union and its citizens. The sanctions against Austria have made this gap much deeper. In recent weeks and months, the Austrian Federal Government has not only received great support from the Austrian population, which has been expressed in many personal encounters and countless letters – thanking ourselves sincerely and with all its heart – but Austria has also experienced enormous support from people from all over Europe who know our country and who were deeply outraged by the unjust accusations and sanctions against Austria. [[Congratulations on the Free and the ÖVP]] The fact that the imposition of these sanctions took place almost overnight, without reference to the European Parliament rather than national parliaments, is a reflection of a very serious democratic deficit in the European Union. It must not be surprising that people in Austria and Europe feel that they are being decided across their heads in Brussels. It should also be no wonder that citizens lose faith in the high ideals of integration when a foreign minister of an EU country says: "My goal is to overthrow the Austrian government!"if another Austrian government party calls it a "hairy beast in the needle strip" and if a third party says: "I will do the devil and apologize to Austria!" [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] Should we really imagine the political elites of Europe? Should this be the spokesperson of a new Europe? [[Inter-calls at the SPÖ.]] Is this the language that Europe will and will expand? – I ask You are very serious, ladies and gentlemen, because I doubt this very much, and I am doing so with many, not only in Austria, but all over Europe. [[New congratulations to the liberals and the ÖVP.]] I am very much in favour of critically dealing with the language in politics, but the distribution of censorships to others is not particularly credible then, Mr Gusenbauer, Mr Edlinger and others from the SPÖ Group, if you are constantly using the verbal threshold. [[Inter-calls at the SPÖ]] The same applies to the European level. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] The measuring with two dimensions, ladies and gentlemen of the opposition, is not a moral category with which you can impress. [[New congratulations to the liberals and the ÖVP.]] To request a report on the situation in Austria is good and beautiful. We have nothing to hide and we don't need to hide. That is why it is only logical that the report of the three wise men not only concluded that Austrian integration policy can easily keep up with the European level, but also that minority policy in Austria and the standards in this area are far above the European level. Fire flags against foreigners, man-hunting against refugees, instigation of cemeteries and synagogues, Nazi tumours and street-slaughters: All this is sad European reality today – but it is not reality in Austria! [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] This federal government – I can assure you – will do everything in its power to ensure that Austria remains peaceful and secure for everyone living in that country. Therefore, vigilance is very appropriate. We will observe very carefully all the developments in Europe. We will be very careful to ensure that fundamental and freedom rights, as well as the basic principles of modern constitutional states, namely democracy, the rule of law and the separation of power, are not excluded from the discussion on European values. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Parent]] But that also means quite clearly to me that there must be more participation for the citizens of Europe. When the EU Commissioner for Enlargement Verheugen recently proposed to use direct-democratic instruments in large and far-reaching decisions such as Easter enlargement, collective outrage of the so-called political elites followed. It is not possible, according to the almost unanimous opinion of the public, to give the people such a complicated decision – as if European integration policy were a kind of secret science, with which only professionals and technocrats can deal. I, ladies and gentlemen, would be very careful in qualifying for what is done by the citizens and what is not. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] It is not only about enlargement of the East, but in principle that there can be no question in principle which cannot be put to the public for a decision. Every decision is reasonable to the citizen – and above all the truth is reasonable to the citizens. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] It is interesting in this context that there has also been support for the Verheugen proposal from a side of which one would not have imagined, such as Oskar Lafontaine, who until recently belonged to the socialist leadership cell in Europe, and from the former German President Roman Herzog. In principle, I think it is positive that this has brought about a debate that has been dealt with for years only as a marginal issue of the Union, but which is a central question for me, namely the elimination of the EU's blatant democratic deficit and the implementation of the principle of subsidiarity in the interests of citizens. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] This discussion must be brought to the centre at a new beginning of Europe, if one is serious about it with one another. And if we think it seriously with one another in Europe, this also means that we have to create the basis for decisions that are credible and comprehensible to the citizens, for example with regard to enlargement of the East. In the interests of Austria, but also in the interests of the Union, there must be a careful assessment of the advantages of what disadvantages are faced, the benefits of which costs. "The method currently applied," writes Christian Ortner in the "Format", "to request a blank check from the taxpayer and to denotify all those who do not wish to sign it will ultimately prove counterproductive." We in Austria see our commitment to strongly support our neighbours in achieving the standards necessary for accession in social policy, environmental policy, legal policy. But we also expect the candidate countries to comply with the standards of human rights and nuclear safety in Europe. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] In this sense, the experience of the last months should be used to share the path of reason. In order to fulfil this path with life and to make fruitful use of it, we must meet with mutual respect, dispute our democratic reliability and place joint responsibility for Austria over all party policy objectives. Given the reaction of the opposition to the abolition of sanctions over the last few days, I have had my legitimate doubts as to whether all the correct lessons have really been learned from this decision. [[Intrusion of the Abg. Huber.]] – Mrs Colleague! Those who want to cover their own bad conscience in connection with the sanctions, by deliberately and completely unfoundedly trying to undermine the political and personal integrity of the Minister of Justice, have not learned the right lessons from the events of recent months. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] Minister of Justice Dr. Dieter Böhmdorfer not only owns my personal trust, but also the full trust of the entire federal government and the trust of the Austrian population, as we know from many reactions of the last days and weeks. [[Congratulations with the liberals and the ÖVP.]] [[Inter-calls at the SPÖ.]] The political conclusions from the sanctions of the "EU-14" states are obvious: Europe needs more citizens' participation. Europe needs a guarantee that democratic elections are respected and respected by citizens. Europe needs institutional arrangements to ensure these objectives. Europe needs a vision and a reflection on its ideals. Europe needs state politicians who, beyond their personal ambition, do not lose sight of Europe's overall interest, state politicians who are equipped with the willingness to respond courageously and determinedly to the challenges of this continent. If we are willing to represent the interests of Austria together in this House, we will not only give a sign of the democratic maturity of this country, but also a sign that we will be a constructive co-designer of the future modern and solidarity Europe. - Thank you.

To this end, legal action is necessary, and all the experts we have heard are aware of the need for such legal provisions. We are basically in agreement with the two government groups that these measures should be put in place. Differently, only the position is how we do it. It is due to the fact that partially unfounded fears have been exposed to animals – and this can pose a danger – that the animal shelters are over, and the dogs who belong to them as dangerous breeds are hardly yet to be brought to the man or woman. In this context, too, I think it would be time for the communities that actually have the task of rising up for these animals under the Constitution to be under the arms of at least the animal welfare organisations, because this miscarriage has been politically caused and co-indebted. [[Abg. Mühlbachler: But also from the Greens!]] – By individual statements. In any case, it is the task of the communities, and they should fulfil it. As far as the debate in the Committee is concerned – and this has calmed me a little bit – it was at least so that the completely consistent opinion of the experts was taken into account. This means that it is apparently not planned to introduce any dog breed bans, because it was clear from the outset that this is not so clearly defined, and it has also been made equally clear that it is human behaviour that can be more or less responsible. It must be assumed: in breeding, in importing, in the attitude and also in the labelling of animals, in order to be able to determine and perceive responsibilities, even if something happens. The applications that have now been submitted – at least in my eyes – remain a great drops of wormwood. In the end, I agree with the request made by the government parties because, at least in some areas, it provides for a uniform federal regime, as far as labelling is concerned, with regard to imports, including the possibilities of the executive, to deal with unscrupulous holders. But it remains a wormhead drop, because the example of the dogs really makes it clear that the only thing that would really be said – and for all animals – would be a uniform system for the protection of animals and humans, but there is still no necessary majority to do so. I find that sad and sad. [[Congratulation with the greens.]] It is also sad and sad that, in the end, the government parties have brought together only one motion for a resolution with calls to the Minister of Justice, the Federal Ministers for Finance and Internal Affairs and the Federal Government. He goes, as I said, in the core, in the right direction in sub-areas, but I'm already asking myself a question: If not even the government parties can present their own legislative text, then it is very clear that this also refers to a deficit in equipment by Parliament and, on the other hand, to an oversupply of the government. There are the legitimate capacities, we do not have them, and I think it is already a certain evidence of poverty, if this House does not draw up legislative proposals itself, but appeals to the ministers: Do this for us! However, I hope, Minister of the Interior, that the legislative proposals you are now preparing will, in line with the majority wish of this House, include experts from the field of animal welfare and also opposition politicians in the deliberations, because our today's approval of this motion for a resolution does not, of course, mean yes and Amen in any legislative text, no matter what comes out. Finally, on the proposal of the Socialist Group in the Committee. I did not agree to this request in the Committee because, although it was in itself the basic philosophy, which is the only right one in our eyes, namely, a federal uniform rule, but then in two main areas of my opinion is inadequate or was – I can almost say now – in the point that the regulations or any prohibitions should again be based on racial characteristics – as I said, that is contrary to expert opinion – and secondly, in the point that in a very undifferentiated sense, all psychologically ill, addictional and vulnerable persons should be banned from the attitude of potentially dangerous dogs. I think that's too far. Especially in certain diseases dogs can also be therapy, really companions in the best sense. I believe that this wording was somewhat inconceivable, and I now also hear at the level of the speakers that there is a willingness here to improve and correct these two sections or these two points until the next negotiations in the Subcommittee. In this sense, as I have now given the government a little pre-confidence on this point, I would also like to do with the Socialist Group because, as has been said, the actual, the federal system is very strongly emphasized in this proposal. In other words, it may seem somewhat paradoxical at first glance, but we will agree with these two proposals.

Since then, other principles have been taken into account in the criminal process in addition to truth finding. The focus of these new principles was on the child's well-being. Among other things, there has also been a restriction on the obligation to display advertising. Since then, § 84 provides that you are no longer obliged to display if – I quote – "the advertisement would affect an official activity whose effectiveness requires a personal relationship of trust". The representatives of the authorities and public services, which have been enabled to do so, were, of course, obliged, in the course of the 1993 revision of the Criminal Procedure, to take a firm interest, with careful consideration in each individual case between victim protection, effective victim assistance and prosecution. This restriction, which was reached in 1993, has proved very positive in practice and has actually exceeded all the expectations that we formulated at the time. So it has fulfilled its purpose. On the one hand, everyone knows from the practice that the victims and the victims can also make greater use of assistance, because this information is confidential and they do not have to fear that, if they have sought help, they may have to re-explain traumatic proceedings in court. Secondly, this has also led to the awareness of the professional groups concerned. Mr Stoisits has already pointed out that these two positive aspects have been on the table since 1989 at the latest, namely since the work of the follow-up research group on the reform of the advertising obligation in the Medical Law. This means, in concrete terms, that the current regulation of § 84 has proved itself in practice. This has been expressed by the majority in the assessment procedure, and we therefore do not think that it is useful to change it. The change in the law planned by the blue-black government will in reality not mean any improvement in the protection of victims, rather it will cause the high level of trust that has been put into social work in recent years to disappear or lead to distrust. In view of this, it is clear to me and to the Socialist Group that this new regime is by no means for the protection of victims, but only for prosecution, and therefore we will not agree to it.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-09-20-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00036_d7e22457	The next speaker is Mr Heinzl. Voluntary speaking time limit: 5 minutes. - Please.
ParlaMint-AT_2000-09-20-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00036_d7e22478	Mr President. Minister! Ladies and gentlemen! High house! International travel activity has increased sharply in recent years, and the tourist attractiveness of the Caribbean island of Cuba has recently caused many Austrians to travel to Cuba. In winter, many Austrians enjoy the sandy beaches and the clean sea on this island. Unfortunately, in some cases bilateral contacts have a criminal dimension. Finally, two cases of hardship, of which Austrians were affected in Cuba, were the occasion for Austria's approach to the Cuban authorities to conclude an agreement on the mutual enforcement of judgments in criminal matters. The experts from both countries then developed a relevant contract, and the draft was drawn up on 14 November. ==References====External links== This treaty now enables condemned people from Austria from Cuba to pay the penalty imposed on them in their home country. This is in the interests of both the convicted person, who can certainly expect more unfavourable circumstances in Cuba in prison than is the case in Austria, for example, but also in the interest of Austrian society, because the reintegration of the convicted person into society is easier if he takes his punishment in Austria. The executing State shall, in principle, impose the term of imprisonment imposed in the executing State, except where the maximum penalty provided for in the executing State is exceeded. The executing State is also bound by the facts which led the State of judgment to the decision taken. In principle, however, the resulting dismissal is governed by the law of the executing State. Ladies and gentlemen! High house! I believe that support should also be given to this Treaty between the Republic of Austria and the Republic of Cuba for humanitarian reasons, and my group will fully endorse this proposal. Thank you.

One school believes that if possible the perpetrator should be left in the family circle for as long as possible, because the family should be treated as a unit. It always happens that the punishing father or stepfather is again sexually aggressive against the child. – The second school says that the perpetrator should be removed from the family association as soon as possible so that the victim can be treated as quickly as possible. – The truth will probably lie in the middle. Ladies and gentlemen! Psychotherapists and social workers have said in agreement that in all cases the following is necessary: If you want to treat the victim or try therapy, then the victim's guilt has to be turned on. As a rule, the girls, minor girls, but also the boys feel guilty when they have become victims of sexual abuse. As long as the blame is not removed from the victim, therapy is not possible. Ladies and gentlemen! The culprit is still not displayed, and the social workers are now doing something that I find really shameless: They play detectives, hear the culprit and say literally: If you don't confess, we'll bring the case to the public. – And to the girl you say: If you don't tell us what's going on, you won't be fine. Ladies and gentlemen! It is precisely these facts that make professional assistance necessary. I have spoken to several social workers today, and I believe that this regulation, which we will decide today, is compatible with the general public with which the social workers and, above all, the victims can live very well. We shall vote in favour of this request. Thank you.

ParlaMint-AT_2000-09-20-021-XXI-NRSITZ-00036_d7e22844	Mr President. Mrs. State Secretary! Ladies and gentlemen! The report on the income situation of women and men in self-employed employment is a very valuable document, even though it presents a very sobering result. The income differences between women and men remain on average around 28 percent, and the causes of this are to be found in many dimensions. I will now present the most important ones to me and then try to derive consequences from them. First and foremost, the choice of profession that meets a person in his life is crucial. It should be noted that women prefer to work in low-paid industries or, in other words, at least as much as they do, that in the sectors where women in particular work, low wages dominate. The second point is education. In this report, it is clear and clear that education minimises income differences, which is a particularly important instrument for reducing income differences. A very interesting detail from my point of view is that in the initial income there is a difference between men and women at the expense of women, which can only be referred to as a "sex discount" which employers make in the classification of women. A particularly important point is that occupational interruptions have a very dramatic impact on the income situation. This report shows that a five-year career interruption results in a loss of income of minus 9 percent. In contrast, a woman who remains in her job in these five years can improve income by plus 20 percent. That is, the difference then sums up to almost 30 percent in these five years. Another point I would like to address in this context is the uneven distribution of supply work, which, of course, is at the expense of women. In order to illustrate the slowness of development and the complexity that is necessary for this purpose, I would like to briefly describe the scenario of how the process could be driven forward, which is presented in this report. It is assumed that there is a consensus on reducing income inequality by one-fifth within ten years. If one were to agree on such a goal in Austria, it would be achievable if the career opportunities for at least two-thirds of the starting women were adapted to those of their male colleagues each year and if in half of the households in which a child is born, mother and father decided to find a similar balance between work performance and personal care. – You see, it would be a very ambitious scenario, and yet we would only make a small progress in these years. What conclusions can be drawn from this report? – On the one hand, the conclusions that the Federal Government is now taking point in the wrong direction. In the field of education – in order to address them – which would be an important instrument on this issue, the introduction of study fees will again force the discussion whether girls with higher basic education really get the chance to complete this education. And with your model of child money, you will create an incentive system for long career interruptions and a system that will consolidate the role models and the role breakdowns. All of this is contrary to the need to divide supply work. The government parties have obviously decided to set the course in the wrong direction and to put in place measures that will further increase income differences. After reading this report, I have to say: Sage Please don't. You didn't do it knowingly.

This report deals with the observation period from 1977 to 1996, is presented by Mr Bartenstein, Minister of State, but was initiated by Mrs Hostasch. This report is distinguished by the breakdown and information. However, it is no less depressing than all the reports that have dealt with this issue, considering that women's income or the demand for equal pay for equal work was actually one of the three central demands of women at the beginning of the women's movement, i.e. 170 years ago. The right to equal training has been achieved, the right to vote has been achieved, but it is extremely difficult to enforce or achieve the same wage for equivalent work. There are some priorities that have already been defined or shown from the outset: for example, the first election. It always surprises me – we have not only taken the legal precautions – that behavioural changes are ultimately more difficult than we have imagined. We insist that women see training and education as a very essential aspect of their life-forming and do not think that they can ultimately fight their lives through any family care obligations to the end of life. There one often still manages the first best job or the first best job in the career election, in order to be in a spatial connection with parents or later with one's own family. At the end of the day, however, in the event of a low income situation, with a low qualification, the spiral goes down, up to retirement, so that pension provision is much lower. The fact is that, obviously – and the authors have noticed this – the legal regulations are relatively far-reaching, that there are no longer many possibilities and that developments can always be seen only in a period of observation of about ten years. I therefore hope that all that we have done in the National Employment Plan for Women and, of course, what is not yet apparent here will ultimately show positive results in a next report. Mrs Kuntzl! We make family and profession compatible and stand for the elimination of female-discriminatory working time schemes as well as for promotions during the family phase and for re-entry. [[Congratulations on the ÖVP and on representatives of the Free Trade Unions.]] We are committed to the flexibility of working time and the promotion of highly qualified part-time jobs. I thank all the women for their great achievements for society and for the family! [[Congratulations and bravo reputation with the ÖVP and the liberals.]] My very much appreciated Ladies and gentlemen! Let me make another statement. Allow me to say that women's affairs are unfortunately always much too limited. Women's affairs concern every policy area. The new government has done good in all areas so far, and this good and swift work is especially beneficial for women! Thank you. [[Congratulations and bravo reputation with the ÖVP and with representatives of the liberals.]] In this context, I call the equality between workers and employees, the tax reform, the reduction of rental and housing costs and the excellent economic growth and the reduction of unemployment: 14 000 women are less unemployed. Child rearing times are designed for pension purposes, and we are particularly committed to creating more and new childcare facilities.
